#3028. We May Be Witnessing Wall Street's Biggest Bubble Burst in Real Time (and I'm Not Talking About Artificial Intelligence)

The stock market’s $1.5 trillion powder keg may be about to blow.

By Sean Williams – Nov 21, 2026 at 9:06AM EST

10 stocks we like better than S&P 500 Index ›

Since the 2022 bear market bottomed, it's been all systems go for the ageless Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -1.93%), benchmark S&P 500 (^GSPC -3.70%), and innovation-inspired Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -5.28%). Since reaching their respective troughs, the Dow has powered higher by up to 90%, while the S&P 500 and Nasdaq have more than doubled.

While the artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure build-out has been the primary catalyst behind Wall Street's gains, it's not the only reason stocks are higher. Historically low peak marginal corporate income tax rates have fueled record share buyback activity by S&P 500 companies. Stock-split euphoria and record-breaking initial public offerings are also intriguing retail investors.

While history has repeatedly shown that the Dow, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite rally over multiple decades, it also tells us that bull markets don't last forever. Eventually, something is going to knock Wall Street's AI-driven bull market off its pedestal -- and we may be watching that bubble burst before our very eyes right now.

While everyone's focused on the potential formation and subsequent bursting of an AI bubble, there's a $1.5 trillion bubble that looks to be bursting in real time.

Even though past events can't concretely predict the future, history has a way of rhyming on Wall Street. In other words, historical precedent often sets the tone.

Over the last three decades, few, if any, past events, metrics, or indicators have accurately forecast the future quite like outstanding margin debt.

Margin represents the money an investor borrows, with interest, from their broker to short-sell (wager against) or purchase securities, such as stocks or exchange-traded funds (ETFs). When used to buy stocks or ETFs, margin acts as leverage -- and leverage can be risky.

On the one hand, if a stock or ETF moves in the desired direction, margin can help an investor amplify their gains. But if the security in question moves in the opposite direction, it can quickly magnify losses, on top of owing the broker interest on what's borrowed. This dynamic is what makes outstanding margin debt a crude but incredibly useful risk-measuring metric.

Over multiple decades, we'd expect outstanding margin debt to rise in lockstep with the cumulative value of the U.S. stock market. But when margin debt rises in parabolic fashion over a short period, things typically break.

Total Margin Debt hits $1.5 Trillion, a new all-time high 🤯 👀 https://t.co/1IXqZGgrqs

Since 1998, outstanding margin debt has skyrocketed at least 65% over a short timeline on four occasions, the previous three of which were followed by significant stock market declines:

While the AI revolution is fueling risk-taking, we're witnessing possible evidence of a top. Over the previous four months, outstanding margin debt has fallen from $1.502 trillion to $1.448 trillion, as of October.

When investors lighten up their leverage following a parabolic increase, it's historically been the ultimate red flag for Wall Street and investors that a bubble is about to burst.

Though history doesn't always offer the rosiest of short-term outlooks, historical precedent can change drastically depending on investors' perspectives.

In one respect, corrections and bear markets are par for the course when putting your money to work in the stock market. Given that emotions often drive downturns, there isn't much that can be done from a monetary or fiscal policy perspective to stop these double-digit declines from cropping up.

But these downturns share a theme: they're historically short-lived.

Six months ago, the analysts at wealth management firm Bespoke Investment Group calculated the calendar-day length of every S&P 500 bull and bear market since the start of the Great Depression in September 1929. They uncovered a night-and-day difference between optimism and pessimism on Wall Street.

The current bull market that began on 10/12/22 is now the 9th longest in S&P 500 history, surpassing the 1,324-day bull that ended on 2/9/1966: https://t.co/4mGsS2t2ft

Whereas the typical S&P 500 bear market has lasted just 286 calendar days (about 9.5 months) over the last 97 years, the average bull market has persisted for approximately 3.6 times as long (1,023 calendar days).

Furthermore, no bear market has taken longer than 630 calendar days to find its trough, according to Bespoke. Meanwhile, just over half (14 of 27) of the S&P 500 bull markets since September 1929 have lasted longer than 630 calendar days.

There isn't a single predictive tool that can tell investors precisely when a stock market downturn will begin, how long it'll last, or how steep the decline will be. But history does conclusively show that these downturns have always represented buying opportunities for optimistic, long-term-minded investors. If the margin debt bubble does burst, opportunity will await.

#3026. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#3024. Prediction: This Will Be Micron’s Stock Price by the End of 2028

Is Micron's low valuation an attractive entry point?

By Adam Levy – Nov 21, 2026 at 9:15AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Micron Technology ›

Few companies have seen their earnings soar amid the artificial intelligence boom like Micron Technology (MU -13.89%). The memory chipmaker has benefited from a spike in demand for its chips, even as it and its competitors take years to ramp up new supply capacity. The result is eye-popping profit margins and a stock price that's gone parabolic.

The stock trades for about $690 per share as of this writing, an impressive 201% gain over the past year and a 598% gain over the past 18 months. Even so, the stock is about 43% off its all-time high reached over the summer.

But as the saying goes, "Past results are not indicative of future returns." Future potential investors need to consider what they can expect from Micron and what that means for the stock price. Here's my prediction for Micron's stock price by the end of 2028.

Micron's earnings growth is directly tied to its ability to charge extremely high prices for memory, specifically high bandwidth memory (HBM), which gets packaged with GPUs and AI accelerators for AI training and inference.

As a result, Micron's gross margin has climbed to an astonishing 87%. But that pricing isn't indicative of a competitive moat, as many high-gross-margin businesses exhibit. Note that rival chipmaker SK Hynix (SKHY -13.11%) produced a gross margin of 86% in its most recent quarter, practically on par with Micron.

The margin strength stems from the supply/demand imbalance. Micron said supply constraints will be even tighter in 2027 and 2028 than they were in 2026 in its fourth-quarter earnings presentation. That suggests prices could keep climbing through 2028.

SK Hynix suggested that demand could outpace supply through 2030. But just because demand remains higher than supply doesn't mean prices will keep climbing. Demand has to grow faster than supply.

So, investors expecting prices to continue climbing through 2030 may be mistaken. Micron, SK Hynix, and Samsung are set to open and ramp up new production facilities in late 2028.

So, it's likely pricing peaks in 2028 ahead of the massive ramp-up in production capacity. Earnings could peak in late 2028 or early 2029 as a result.

Importantly, as supply constraints ease, prices go down, gross margins decline, and operating costs increase. Once supply reaches equilibrium or exceeds demand, profits can sink quickly. And given the massive amounts memory chipmakers are spending on new capacity, the next downcycle could lead to a big drop in earnings.

The potential for that drop will affect how much investors are willing to pay for the stock's earnings near the cycle peak. A bigger drop-off in earnings will result in a lower multiple on the stock. Micron and its competitors are working to reduce the cyclicality of their businesses by signing long-term agreements with customers, but that may only delay an earnings decline and make the drop-off worse once those contracts expire.

As such, investors are unlikely to pay a very high multiple near Micron's peak earnings. Historically, Micron has commanded a multiple of 3-8 times earnings at its peak. With the potential for a large downcycle, shares are likely to trade in the 3-5-times range, with earnings expected to peak in 2028 or 2029.

It's important to note that the stock price usually peaks before earnings peak. Investors are almost always forward-looking. Any hint that pricing is nearing its peak and earnings are set for a near-term downturn will push the stock lower. We saw that happen last month when Sandisk reported disappointing price improvements for its flash memory NAND chips, and the stock dropped after earnings.

With analysts currently expecting $211 per share in earnings for fiscal 2028 and $218 per share for fiscal 2029, that puts my prediction for Micron's stock price at the end of 2028 somewhere between $610 and $1,040. With the stock trading for $690 right now, it doesn't look like a great time to buy unless you think the earnings cycle will last much longer and the drop-off in earnings will be far less severe once earnings peak.

#3020. CME vs. Robinhood Markets: Which Financial Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

Robinhood's revenue grew far faster than CME's over the latest 12 months, yet its debt load tells a very different story about financial risk.

By Erin Kennedy – Nov 21, 2026 at 9:20AM EST

10 stocks we like better than CME Group ›

Market volatility often creates opportunities for the platforms that facilitate trading. Deciding between CME Group (CME +0.41%) and Robinhood Markets (HOOD -9.44%) involves weighing the stability of an institutional giant against a fast-growing retail disruptor.

CME Group provides the essential infrastructure for global derivatives trading, offering unmatched liquidity to professional investors. Robinhood Markets has transitioned from a simple trading app to a diversified financial services provider for the modern retail investor. Both companies are positioned to benefit from increased market activity but offer different paths for shareholders.

CME Group operates the world's most diverse derivatives marketplace, offering contracts across interest rates, equities, and commodities in 200 countries. Key partnerships include a 10-year agreement with Alphabet (GOOG -2.59%) (GOOGL -2.70%) and a prediction market joint venture with Flutter Entertainment (FLUT -1.80%). Because one company accounted for roughly 12% of clearing and transaction fees in its latest report, this customer concentration adds a layer of risk.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached nearly $7 billion, an increase of approximately 9% over the prior period. During this time, net income was roughly $4.4 billion as the company benefited from increased trading volumes. CME maintains a high net margin of approximately 63.1%, highlighting its strength among financial stocks.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio was approximately 0.1. This ratio compares total debt to shareholder equity, indicating the company uses very little borrowed money to fund its operations. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was nearly $4.2 billion, reflecting strong cash conversion from its earnings.

Robinhood Markets provides retail brokerage, advisory, and digital banking services to millions of funded customers. Its platform supports trading in traditional assets as well as Bitcoin (BTC -2.58%). It also operates a joint venture with Susquehanna International Group to run an independent futures exchange and clearinghouse.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached nearly $5.1 billion, fueled by revenue growth of approximately 21.1%. The company reported net income of close to $2 billion for the same period, reflecting success in scaling its subscription services and interest-earning assets. Its net margin for this time frame was roughly 39%.

As of Robinhood's December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio was approximately 1.3. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow reached nearly $1.6 billion, showing the company's ability to generate cash while expanding.

CME Group faces intense competition from other global exchanges like Intercontinental Exchange (ICE -0.52%) and Nasdaq (NDAQ -0.99%). The company is also subject to regulatory and legislative changes that could impact its clearinghouse and transaction structures. Furthermore, the company manages operational risks related to third-party data centers, highlighted by a cooling failure in November 2025. Cybersecurity threats targeting financial infrastructure also remain a persistent risk for the exchange operator.

Robinhood Markets is sensitive to regulatory scrutiny regarding payment for order flow, which accounts for a substantial portion of its revenue. It competes with established brokerages like Charles Schwab (SCHW -2.13%) and fintech rivals that may have greater capital and brand recognition. The company also depends on a limited number of liquidity providers, such as Virtu Financial (VIRT -4.76%), to execute its trades. Additionally, legal challenges regarding the offering of event contracts could impact Robinhood's future product road map.

Robinhood Markets is cheaper by its P/S ratio, which measures market cap against sales over the past 12 months, while CME Group has a lower forward P/E.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

It's a little hard to pick one over the other because of the current macro environment. Specifically, the market is, as the youths say, crashing out. Robinhood stock is less than a dollar away from its 52-week low. I mean, this year-to-date chart is brutal:

Its stock is also a lot more volatile than CME Group's. Robinhood has seen stronger revenue growth recently, but CME posts a consistently higher profit margin. (This chart's a little wonky because Robinhood didn't go public until 2021.)

In addition to CME's better profitability, I like that it pays a modest dividend yielding about 1.7%.

I think in a somewhat unsettled market like the one we're currently experiencing, a mature, established operator looks like a more attractive stock. Some investors may prefer growth over consistency, in which case, Robinhood may be more appealing. But for me, CME Group gets my vote for 2027 and beyond.

#3015. ASML vs. Broadcom: Which Tech Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

ASML's near-monopoly on chip-printing machines faces off against Broadcom's networking empire, with a sharp valuation gap complicating the choice for growth investors.

By Erin Kennedy – Updated Nov 21, 2026 at 9:21AM EST

The semiconductor world is currently split between the machines that build chips and the designs that power them. Is ASML (ASML -6.63%) or Broadcom (AVGO -12.19%) the better play for your portfolio?

ASML holds a virtual monopoly on the advanced lithography machinery required to print the world's most sophisticated microchips. Meanwhile, Broadcom has evolved into a powerhouse of networking components and enterprise software. Both companies are critical to the tech supply chain, but they offer distinct paths for investors seeking exposure to high-end silicon.

ASML manufactures lithography systems, which are massive machines that use light to print intricate patterns onto silicon wafers. It provides the essential hardware for every major chipmaker to produce smaller, faster, and more efficient processors. Because its technology is unique, it serves as a critical gatekeeper for the entire high-end semiconductor market.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached nearly $43.4 billion, an increase of roughly 22.5%. The company generated approximately $14.8 billion in net income. This growth highlights the sustained demand for extreme ultraviolet lithography systems used in advanced chip production for artificial intelligence and high-performance computing.

As of ASML's December 2025 balance sheet, the current ratio, which measures a company's ability to cover its short-term debts with short-term assets, is approximately 1.3. The debt-to-equity ratio is roughly 0.2, showing that the company uses relatively little debt compared to its total equity. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was close to $12.2 billion.

Broadcom is a dominant player among semiconductor stocks, providing infrastructure software and hardware solutions to global enterprises. The company has significant customer concentration, with its top five end customers accounting for roughly 40% of revenue in its latest annual report. Customer concentration like this adds a layer of risk to the business, as evidenced by a $30 billion commitment from Apple (AAPL -1.90%).

In the 12 months ended Aug. 2, 2026, revenue reached close to $89.1 billion, representing significant growth of nearly 48.7%. The company reported net income of approximately $38.3 billion during this time frame. This surge was partly driven by the integration of large acquisitions and booming demand for artificial intelligence infrastructure across data centers.

As of Broadcom's November 2025 balance sheet, the current ratio is roughly 1.7. The debt-to-equity ratio is approximately 0.8, indicating a higher level of total debt relative to shareholder equity than ASML. In the fiscal year ended Nov. 2, 2025, free cash flow was close to $26.9 billion, though stock-based compensation accounted for roughly 27.5% of operating cash flow, inflating reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

ASML faces risks from its position at the center of global trade tensions, particularly concerning international export restrictions. The company also operates in the cyclical semiconductor equipment market, where spending by chipmakers can fluctuate based on broader economic conditions and interest rates. Furthermore, the rapid pace of innovation requires constant, heavy investment in research and development to maintain its technological lead over any potential competitors.

Broadcom operates in a highly cyclical industry that faces rapid price erosion and evolving technological requirements driven by artificial intelligence. It relies heavily on a limited number of contract manufacturers, such as Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company (TSM -9.48%), which produced approximately 95% of its wafers according to its latest annual report. Broadcom also faces intense regulatory scrutiny and risks associated with its strategy of integrating large acquisitions into its software portfolio.

Broadcom looks potentially cheaper based on its forward P/E, which compares the stock price to future earnings estimates, whereas ASML has a lower P/S ratio.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

The current market environment is, shall we say, hostile to tech stocks, and shares of ASML and Broadcom reflect that:

That said, zooming out over the past decade, both stocks have put in decent performances, handily outpacing the S&P 500's (^GSPC -3.70%) return:

In the past, I have picked ASML over many other stocks for several reasons. For one, it has a monopoly in the extreme ultraviolet lithography niche it operates in. That gives it strong pricing power and a solid moat. ASML also consistently reports a net margin in excess of 30%, and it pays a modest dividend.

However, it's hard to argue with Broadcom's long-term performance. A company of that size reporting nearly 50% revenue growth is pretty wild. ASML and Broadcom are both exposed to the inherent cyclicality of the semiconductor industry, so I think that risk factor offsets itself. And the tech sector (and the market in general) may be melting down right now, but long-term investors shouldn't get especially concerned over what happens in the course of a week, or a month, or even a year.

I'm breaking with my pattern here. I don't think ASML is a bad choice, to be clear, but Broadcom has strong margins and even better revenue growth. Broadcom gets my vote for 2027 and beyond.

#3012. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#3011. Not Anthropic. Not SpaceX. This Is the AI Stock to Buy for the Long Term

Under-the-radar chip stock On Semiconductor trades at a compelling value, and the company has impressive long-term growth prospects.

By Lee Samaha – Nov 21, 2026 at 9:22AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

The Anthropic (ANT -8.50%) initial public offering (IPO) this week reinforced the view that, despite recent turbulence related to developments at Nvidia, investment in artificial intelligence (AI) remains in high-growth mode. That's not in doubt; still, the key question for retail investors is how to find the best-value way to invest in it. Companies like the AI lab and Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX -7.69%), with its AI infrastructure, rocket launch, and connectivity businesses, appear attractive on this basis. Still, I think there's another company that's a better buy on a risk-adjusted basis: On Semiconductor (ON -7.42%).

To buy into the investment theme, you need to believe that the use of AI applications will continue to grow, spurring growth for AI enterprises like Anthropic and, in turn, demand for SpaceX's data center infrastructure. While these assumptions about the outlook for end demand seem entirely feasible, the reality is that both companies are engaged in a fiercely competitive, highly capital-intensive arms race.

In Anthropic's case, it needs to continually and heavily invest in leasing compute capacity from infrastructure services (including those from SpaceX) to compete for frontier model supremacy with its rivals -- a group that actually includes SpaceX. Anthropic has already made over $900 billion in spending commitments, including $120.5 billion in an agreement with SpaceX.

In turn, compute providers like SpaceX and the well-known hyperscalers are having to make massive investments into new data center capacity to meet their contractual obligations. These investments are not risk-free. In SpaceX's case in particular, there are serious questions about how quickly its planned orbital data center satellites will depreciate. SpaceX also faces launch risk every time it attempts to send any cargo into space, and if it merges with Tesla, the company will be part of a much larger and more complex business.

All told, if there's any slowdown in demand for AI applications, both Anthropic and SpaceX are likely to suffer disproportionately.

The power and sensing chip company is largely a play on the increasing power density, not only in its core automotive and industrial end markets, but also in its fast-growing data center business. The company is best known for power control chips and sensing technology that are used in electric vehicles (EVs), as well as myriad industrial applications ranging from EV charging and industrial automation to renewable energy and consumer electronics.

As an investment, On Semiconductor is not without risk. Its core automotive business is exposed to the cyclicality of the EV market; the power semiconductor market is competitive, and there's execution risk around its recent AI-related acquisitions. However, the diversity of its end markets means it doesn't face the same degree of existential risk as companies like Anthropic and SpaceX do from frontier model competition and capital-intensive investments.

In contrast to many AI-related companies, On Semiconductor is highly cash-generative, with Wall Street analysts targeting $2 billion in free cash flow (FCF) for 2027 and $2.3 billion for 2028, according to Visible Alpha. Based on its current market cap of $25.1 billion, the stock would be trading at 12.6 times FCF in 2027 and 10.9 times FCF in 2028.

Those are highly attractive valuations for any company whose management is targeting 30% annual operating income growth from 2026 to 2030. In that period, its data center revenue growth is expected to grow at a 50% annual rate.

On top of all of this, On Semiconductor's power and sensing solutions are set to become a key feature of physical AI solutions (such as robots, EVs, drones, and industrial machines) over the long term. As more physical AI products are developed, there will be an increasing need for edge power density. For example, robots can't wait a few seconds for calculations in the cloud; they need to process sensory inputs in real time, which requires power and sensing chips.

As such, the company has significant exposure to a major AI investment theme, but doesn't trade at a nosebleed valuation, and doesn’t carry the kind of downside risk inherent in other pure-play AI stocks.

#3009. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

#2991. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2988. Rams WR Puka Nacua gets knee surgery, likely out for season

Rams WR Puka Nacua gets knee surgery, likely out for season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

All-Pro receiver Puka Nacua is likely to miss the rest of the season after undergoing knee surgery, Los Angeles Rams coach Sean McVay said Friday.

Nacua injured ligaments in his knee while carrying the ball on the final offensive snap of the Rams' win over the Washington Commanders on Nov. 8.

McVay had expressed optimism last week that Nacua could return to the field this season, but he said the star wideout decided this week that he should have knee surgery, which was performed Tuesday.

Nacua received a three-game suspension from the NFL for violating its personal conduct policies on Thursday. His suspension will expire during his mandated stint on injured reserve, but the knee surgery will keep him out much longer.

McVay didn't rule out a return by Nacua during the playoffs, but he wouldn't speculate that far ahead.

Nacua has been one of the most productive receivers in NFL history during his first four seasons and has 70 catches for 715 yards and four touchdowns while playing in just seven games this year. He is in the final year of his rookie contract and is eligible to sign a large new deal.

The Rams (6-4), currently outside of the playoff picture, will be without their top two pass-catchers when they host the Green Bay Packers on Wednesday night. Davante Adams also isn't ready to return from his hamstring injury, but McVay is optimistic he can play the following week.

#2985. Jensen Huang's — and Nvidia's — legal exposure balloons

The still-unraveling scandal of former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's extramarital affairs and the tech giant's potential role in its advanced semiconductor chips ending up in China present a legal minefield for Huang and Nvidia.

Federal and state prosecutors and Nvidia employees may target Huang and the company in court, where some shareholders have already taken action.

That's on top of a rapidly growing pile of probes by Republicans who control Congress now and Democrats who will have majorities in both the House and Senate come January.

"It's not going to get any better for Jensen Huang; it's going to get far worse," said Salvatore Graziano, a New York securities litigation attorney at Bernstein, Litowitz, Berger & Grossmann. His firm is looking at shareholder lawsuit opportunities against Nvidia and Huang.

[Huang] "faces the Department of Justice, the Securities and Exchange Commission and private litigation," Graziano said. "The criminal exposure is very serious. The SEC could bar him from being an officer. And the potential liability [from shareholder] lawsuits can be very large."

Huang's and Nvidia's legal exposure stems from three issues that led up to his shock resignation Nov. 12.

There's his alleged affair with suspected Chinese spy Alice Huang, who worked for Megaspeed, a Singapore-based company under investigation for diverting advanced Nvidia semiconductor chips to China in violation of U.S. export rules. That, in turn, has raised questions about whether Huang and Nvidia were aware of any diversions.

There are Huang's two other alleged affairs with Nvidia employees.

And there is the sharp drop in the company's stock price in the wake of the scandal. Nvidia's share price has fallen 19% since Nov. 8, when Huang sent a company-wide apology acknowledging extramarital affairs.

Huang last week retained the law firm Paul, Weiss and its attorneys Melinda Haag and Walter Brown to represent him. Haag is a former U.S. attorney in San Francisco, and Brown is also a former federal prosecutor.

Huang's spokesman at Trident, a crisis communications firm, declined to comment when asked if Haag and Brown are representing him in any potential criminal case and other questions about his legal exposure.

Nvidia did not respond to a request for comment. Last week, Nvidia retained the law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct an independent investigation into Huang allegedly being targeted by a spy, as U.S. intelligence reportedly has concluded.

The civil liability Huang and Nvidia could face is underscored by a hearing Graziano, the securities attorney, attended Tuesday in U.S. District Court in San Francisco. A judge at the hearing approved a $175 million settlement from Seagate Technology Holdings to resolve a shareholder lawsuit alleging it concealed sales of hard-disk drives to Huawei in China in violation of export bans.

The Seagate settlement came three years after the Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security — which is investigating Megaspeed's business with Nvidia — imposed a $300 million penalty on Seagate for its sales to Huawei.

Graziano said his law firm is "actively looking at securities law liability for Nvidia, Jensen Huang and other senior executives in the company given all the recent developments regarding their [potential] knowledge and facilitation of illegal chip sales to Chinese entities."

There is already a pending class-action shareholder lawsuit in San Francisco federal court against Nvidia and Huang by the Rosen Law Firm, for allegedly misleading and false statements related to Megaspeed and Alice Huang.

"Several hundreds of investors have contacted us and expressed an interest to join the case — investors of all walks of life, from mom and pops to investors with very large losses," Rosen attorney Phil Kim told CNBC in an email.

Katie Sweeten, a former federal prosecutor in the DOJ's National Security Division, said the question of "who knew what and when, and what was [Huang's] role" is potentially the biggest risk for Nvidia and him.

"Now DOJ can leverage their criminal authorities," Sweeten, a partner at Scale LLP, told CNBC's Kelly Evans on "The Exchange."

"They can bring grand jury subpoenas, search warrants for emails, bank accounts, personal devices, computers, things like that," Sweeten said.

While it is "not illegal to, you know, sleep with a spy ... it is illegal to pass certain types of information to a counterintelligence officer," she said.

Sweeten also pointed to potential export control violations, which "could be very serious."

She noted the DOJ can indict a company as well as its officials if there is evidence of criminality. Export control violations carry a maximum possible criminal sentence of 20 years in prison and "really large fines," she said.

"You can't send the company to prison, but they can face fines, civil penalties, debarment — where you can't export items down the line," she said.

One example of that came in July 2025, when the DOJ obtained a guilty plea and an agreement to pay more than $140 million from Cadence Design Systems for violating the Export Control Reform Act by unlawfully exporting semiconductor design tools to China's National University of Defense Technology, a school led by China's Central Military Commission.

Another potential legal risk for Huang comes from his planned appearances before Congress.

Huang is scheduled to give a closed-door interview Dec. 1 to three House committees. Senate banking committee Chair Tim Scott, R-S.C., wants Huang's testimony before that panel to be public, and told CNBC Tuesday he hopes to have that happen "before Christmas."

Sweeten said Huang "has to be very careful" about what he says there because of the risk of being criminally charged with lying to Congress, and of saying something "that absolutely can become part of a criminal or civil investigation."

"I expect Huang to take the Fifth on most questions" from Congress, Jonathan Kanter, a former assistant attorney general in the DOJ's Antitrust Division, told CNBC, meaning Huang will likely use his constitutional right against giving testimony that could be used against him in a criminal case.

"With a Justice Department criminal investigation into Megaspeed already underway and more investigations possible, anything he tells Congress could be used against him," Kanter said. "His lawyers are likely to advise him against taking that risk."

Ben Heineman Jr., a former senior vice president and general counsel of General Electric, and a senior fellow at Harvard University, told CNBC that Nvidia's board will be scrutinized over its actions before it pushed Huang to resign.

Heineman cited the board's apparent lack of succession planning and what looks like weak oversight of Huang, including relating to personal conduct such as the two alleged affairs with Nvidia employees and with an executive of an Nvidia customer.

"When there is a sort of reasonable suspicion, they need to investigate it, and they don't appear to have done that," Heineman said.

Nvidia and Huang could face investigation from state attorneys general, including in California, where the company is headquartered.

On Tuesday, California Attorney General Rob Bonta told Bloomberg Television that the situation at Nvidia with Huang is "something that we're interested in." Bonta's office later told CNBC, "We are monitoring the situation."

Huang's alleged affairs with Nvidia employees also open the possibility of workplace litigation.

Ari Wilkenfeld, a Washington-area employment lawyer, said a consensual relationship between a chief executive and a subordinate "is not in and of itself illegal."

"The liability never comes from the existence of the relationship alone. It comes from whether it was genuinely welcome, what job benefits flowed from it, and what it did to everyone else in the building," Wilkenfeld said. "California law recognizes that so-called sexual favoritism can create a hostile environment for an entire workforce.

"The affair is Huang's problem," Wilkenfeld said. "What, if anything, Nvidia did once it knew is the company's problem."

#2984. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2983. Eagles injury update: Offensive reinforcements return after bye week

Eagles injury update: Offensive reinforcements return after bye week originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles are getting some key offensive reinforcements as they return from their Week 10 bye week.

Both DeVonta Smith (hamstring) and Dontayvion Wicks (ankle) were practicing on Wednesday as the Eagles (2-7) began their to prepare to host the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) on Sunday afternoon at the Linc.

Smith and Wicks each missed the last three games before the bye week. The Eagles didn't place them on IR, which signaled a hope that they would be back for the Steelers game. That plan appears to be on track.

"We'll see how that plan unfolds as it goes," head coach Nick Sirianni said. "Like I said, we're hopeful. We'll see how the week goes. I don't want to promise anything, but we'll see how the week goes, how it unfolds and excited to get out there and practice today."

It ought to help quarterback Joe Milton, who will make his second-career start on Sunday, to have the team's top two receivers back on the field with him. During the last three games, rookie Makai Lemon has been the Eagles' leading receiver.

The only player on the 53-man roster who wasn't practicing was Pro Bowl linebacker Zack Baun. He was a spectator on Wednesday with a back injury.

Limited: DeVonta Smith (hamstring), Michael Carter (abdomen), Britain Covey (knee), Jonathan Jones (hamstring), Will Shipley (foot), Nolan Smith (illness), Dontayvion Wicks (ankle)

Full: Saquon Barkley (hamstring), Jihaad Campbell (hamstring/quad), Jonathan Greenard (ankle), Tanner McKee (rib), Smael Mondon (ankle), Drew Mukuba (knee)

A few other players, in addition to Smith and Wicks, also returned to practice after missing the Giants game before the bye: LB Jihaad Campbell (hamstring/quad), DB Michael Carter II (abdomen), WR Britain Covey (knee) and CB Jonathan Jones (hamstring).

Will Shipley also returned to practice on Wednesday. The Eagles opened up his 21-day practice window earlier in the week. Shipley has been on Injured Reserve with a foot injury since Oct. 16. The only running backs on the 53-man roster right now are Saquon Barkley and Jaydon Blue. Shipley was getting reps as a kick returner during the early portion of practice.

The Eagles will release their full injury report later on Wednesday.

An official website of the United States Government

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

#2980. A wave of 'pent-up quits' could boost turnover in the job market, expert says: 'The labor market is a bit like a coiled spring'

Frustrated workers could trigger a wave of turnover, creating a shift in the sluggish job market, according to experts at the job boards Indeed and Glassdoor.

That sluggishness, or what labor economists call a low-hire, low-fire economy, means that fewer people are getting hired, and fewer workers are leaving their jobs — even as a growing share of job holders report frustration with their career opportunities. In 2026, 21% of workers report feeling dissatisfied with their career prospects, according to Glassdoor reviewer data. Dissatisfied workers rated their jobs at a one or two out of five stars.

A stagnant job market can discourage even the unhappiest of workers from quitting, but hiring could soon ramp up. Indeed's Job Posting Index, or JPI, which tracks the level of job postings on Indeed relative to a pre-pandemic baseline, saw year-over-year growth for the first time in September 2026 since 2022. The upward trend suggests that the job market is "heating up," Glassdoor chief economist Daniel Zhao tells CNBC Make It.

As more opportunities open up, job holders might regain the confidence to exit, turning into "a wave of quits and turnover," Zhao says. Turnover could also help those who are out of a job secure employment as roles that were once filled become vacant.

"The labor market is a bit like a coiled spring right now," Zhao said at a press event Tuesday in New York City. "There's a lot of pent-up potential energy, and it's unclear exactly which way that that spring is going to release that energy."

In a healthy job market, employee dissatisfaction can forecast turnover rates. When employee dissatisfaction reached 17% in April 2022, per Glassdoor reviews, quit rates also reached their highest levels at 3%, seasonally adjusted, according to data from Bureau of Labor Statistics Job Openings and Labor Turnover Survey data published by the St. Louis Federal Reserve.

However, when workers feel anxious about their future job prospects, turnover can stall. Though workers feel dissatisfied with their jobs at higher rates in September 2026 than they did in April 2022, only 2.1% of workers quit their job in the month, according to the BLS JOLTS.

"There aren't those opportunities to climb the career ranks internally or externally," Zhao says of the job market. "There's no outlet for the frustration or dissatisfaction that [workers] might be feeling."

Employee turnover was one focus of Indeed and Glassdoor's 2027 Hiring and Worklife Trends Report, published Wednesday. Their research, which assumed a consistent relationship between worker dissatisfaction and resignations based on historical data, found that the quit rate is 0.22% lower than what employee sentiment would typically suggest. While the figure seems small, it is equivalent to an additional 349,000 quits per month.

"Workers are very hesitant to quit, especially without something lined up, because hiring is very sluggish, and long-term unemployment is looming over them," Zhao tells CNBC Make It.

While unemployment levels are stable, people are remaining jobless for longer, according to data from the BLS. Those who face long-term unemployment, job seekers who have been out of work for 27 weeks or more, accounted for 27.6% of all unemployed people in October 2026.

Even if more jobs open, turnover across sectors could vary. Industries that are highly exposed to artificial intelligence like software development, engineering and banking previously dragged Indeed's JPI down, meaning employers in these sectors posted fewer jobs. Indeed's data shows that postings in these sectors have since seen growth, and could result in what experts call "pent-up quits," which occur when dissatisfied workers leave their jobs once market conditions improve. Job posts on Indeed for employers in industries with less AI exposure like healthcare have softened, according to experts.

However, business and professional services, which includes banking, shed 27,000 jobs in October 2026, according to data from ADP Research.

"As we look ahead to 2027, that increase in demand might not necessarily translate into more hires, and it also might not be enough hires to give workers the confidence that they can actually quit their jobs and move on to something better," Zhao says.

Even as the job market appears to be trending in a positive direction, further economic pressure could continue to place a strain on employers and their hiring plans.

"Nobody was really expecting 2026 to come with the geopolitical challenges that have happened and the the impact that has had on energy prices and inflation," Zhao says. "And so there's always the outside chance that something happens in 2027 that derails the job market from the path that it's on."

#2979. Christian Parker stripped of play-calling duties with Cowboys

Christian Parker stripped of play-calling duties with Cowboys originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Cowboys have reportedly stripped play-calling duties from defensive coordinator Christian Parker after 10 games.

Parker, 34, spent the previous two seasons coaching defensive backs with the Eagles before taking the DC job in Dallas, but now veteran coach Mike Pettine will take over play-calling, ESPN reported.

The Eagles face the Cowboys on Thanksgiving in eight days.

In the first meeting of these two teams, the Cowboys won 38-21 and the Eagles were able to gain 321 yards — their second-highest total all season.

The Cowboys brought in the 60-year-old Pettine as a senior defensive assistant back in October and a little over a month later, he's now the play-caller. While Parker is remaining on staff as the Cowboys' DC, it doesn't seem like he's long for that position.

The Eagles tried a mid-season DC switch back in 2023 when they went from Sean Desai to Matt Patricia. That certainly didn't work; the defense got worse after the Week 13 change.

When the Cowboys hired Parker, their hope was that he could stabilize that defense to pair with their explosive offense. That hasn't gone to plan. The Cowboys' defense under Parker ranks 32 in yards allowed and 30th in points allowed. In the Cowboys 46-40 loss to the 49ers in Week 10, they gave up 584 total yards.

While Parker has not found success in Dallas, he was a very good defensive backs coach with the Eagles. He helped the Eagles win a Super Bowl in 2024 and then helped Quinyon Mitchell and Cooper DeJean become All-Pros in Year 2 of their careers.

There are plenty of similarities between Parker and Eagles' OC Sean Mannion. Both were exciting hires this offseason of young, up-and-coming coaches. Both were charged with trying to get their respective units to play at a league-average rate and both have been unable to do it.

#2977. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2974. Forget Anthropic: In Europe, the IPO hype is about a blue lagoon

While investors are fixated on Anthropic's blockbuster stock market debut, a very different IPO is making waves in Europe: Iceland's Blue Lagoon, the operator of one of the world's most famous geothermal spas.

Blue Lagoon Corp. raised roughly 14.3 billion Icelandic kronur ($110 million) through the offering, valuing the Icelandic geothermal spa operator at about $787 million, according to the company's investor presentation.

The Blue Lagoon traces its origins to 1992, when Blue Lagoon Ltd. was founded to study the water in the Blue Lagoon, an open-air pool of warm, milky-blue water set in a black lava field in southwestern Iceland. It grew into a business spanning a spa, hotels and skincare made with ingredients from the lagoon.

Shares will trade under the ticker "BLUE" on one of Europe's smallest stock exchanges, Nasdaq Iceland Main Market, part of the Nasdaq Nordic group, which also operates exchanges in Sweden, Denmark and Finland.

"Wellness is the new luxury, and we know that the wellness economy is growing faster than the economy as a whole, so we have tailwind from that," CEO Sigríður Margrét Oddsdóttir told CNBC on Thursday.

"Now is the right time for us to list," she added. "We are a company that thinks long-term, we have a clear vision for the next decade, and being publicly listed gives investors the opportunity to participate in our journey."

The company's operations are centered on Iceland's Reykjanes Peninsula, where, as well as the Blue Lagoon geothermal spa, it runs the Silica Hotel, the Retreat luxury hotel and spa, and the Michelin-starred Moss restaurant.

It also operates the Kerlingarfjöll Highland Base and Hrauneyjar properties in Iceland's highlands, and sells Blue Lagoon-branded skincare products internationally.

The 34-year-old company has averaged 11% annual revenue growth over the past decade, and next year is expected to be a record, with revenue growing by more than half, Oddsdóttir said.

The company plans to use the raised capital to fund expanding its existing properties, acquiring businesses, and developing new destinations.

In total, nearly 4,000 subscriptions were received in the initial public offering, worth about 15.3 billion kronur, about 7% more than the total size of the offering, the company said, citing strong demand from smaller investors, including retail investors.

Iceland's relatively small stock market had just 27 companies on its main exchange at the end of 2025, according to Nasdaq. The market is dominated by banks, seafood companies and other domestically focused firms.

The global wellness industry reached $7.5 trillion in 2025, up 8.9% from the previous year, according to the Global Wellness Institute. Spending on thermal and mineral springs grew 10.4% over the same period, with the institute forecasting annual growth of nearly 10% for the sector through 2030.

Tourism accounted for nearly 9% of Iceland's GDP in 2025, and is growing, according to Statistics Iceland. The country welcomed around 2.3 million international visitors that year, with the U.S. as its largest source market.

#2973. Eagles castoff Fred Johnson joins Andy Reid on Chiefs practice squad

Eagles castoff Fred Johnson joins Andy Reid on Chiefs practice squad originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Veteran offensive lineman Fred Johnson, released by the Eagles Friday, just added seven wins to his resume.

Johnson has signed with the Chiefs' practice squad, so he goes from a 2-7 team that's lost seven straight games and is buried in last place in the NFC East to a Chiefs team that's 7-2 and tied with the Broncos atop the AFC West.

Johnson, 29, started six games – five at left tackle, one at right tackle – during the Eagles' 2024 Super Bowl championship and then eight last year – seven at right tackle, one at left tackle.

But this year, he was passed over for the right tackle spot when Lane Johnson retired in favor of rookie 3rd-round pick Markel Bell. He played only 24 snaps on offense this year after playing 481 in 2024 and 626 in 2025.

In Kansas City, Johnson joins a team that has the 6th-best odds to win the Super Bowl and is coached by Andy Reid, who has won three of them.

The Chiefs list Johnson as a guard on their roster, although he hasn't played guard regularly since he was with the Buccaneers in 2022.

Johnson entered the NFL as an undrafted rookie with the Bengals in 2019 and spent three years in Cincinnati, starting eight games. After a year in Tampa the Eagles signed him in 2023 and he wound up a valuable backup during the Super Bowl run.

He signed with the Jaguars as a free agent in March of 2025 but when that didn't work the Eagles re-aquired him in a trade just after training camp ended. He hit free agency this past spring before re-signing with the Eagles two weeks into free agency.

In his seven-year career, Johnson has played in 92 games with 23 starts. He's played 1,986 career snaps.

The Eagles haven't won a game since Lane Johnson retired. With both Lane and Fred Johnson now gone, the Eagles' swing tackle becomes John Ojukwu, a former undrafted free agent out of Boise State who started nine games for the Titans from 2023 through 2025. The Titans released Ojukwu five times over that three-year period.

The Eagles signed Ojukwu as a futures player in January, waived him at final cuts, then signed him to the practice squad the next day. They signed him to the 53-man roster on Monday.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.

#2970. Anthropic raises $93.5 billion total as underwriters scoop up ‘greenshoe’ overallotment

Anthropic underwriters have officially exercised their overallotment of shares in the historic initial public offering, bringing the total raised to $93.5 billion, according to a release Friday.

The artificial intelligence company raised $81.3 billion on Wednesday and netted an additional $12.2 billion through the overallotment, surpassing SpaceX, which held a record IPO in June.

Anthropic said it sold an aggregate of 1,127,000,000 shares of its Class A common stock at $83 per share in the offering. It sold 980 million shares on Wednesday and its underwriters had the option to purchase up to an additional 147 million shares by Friday as part of the overallotment, which is commonly referred to as the "greenshoe."

Shares of the Claude maker opened trading at $111.50 on the Nasdaq on Thursday, but slid throughout the day and closed at $97.17. The stock sank 12% in premarket trading on Friday during a broad tech sell-off.

In addition to the sale of its Class A common stock, Anthropic closed a concurrent sale of its Class N common stock after a group of tech companies, including Advanced Micro Devices, Google, Microsoft, Nvidia and others, agreed to participate in a $26 billion private placement at the IPO price. Anthropic said Friday that aggregate gross proceeds from its IPO, including the private placement, totaled $119.5 billion, before deducting offering expenses.

Anthropic dodged an unforgiving series of obstacles on its road to the Nasdaq on Thursday, as investors brooded over Jensen Huang's shocking ouster at Nvidia, voters' stinging rebuke of the tech during the midterms and whether rising interest rates would stymie industry plans for a multitrillion-dollar AI infrastructure buildout.

CEO Dario Amodei co-founded the company in 2021 after he and a handful of other researchers, including his sister Daniela, quit their jobs at OpenAI, now Anthropic's chief rival. Anthropic is best known for developing a family of AI models called Claude, which have captivated the corporate world and fueled the company's historic rise.

The siblings celebrated the IPO as a "milestone" in an interview with CNBC on Thursday, but said there's more work ahead for Anthropic.

"For us, it's like one stop on the journey," Daniela Amodei said. "We think about this as, 'Wow, this is an incredible moment for Anthropic for our mission, but we're going to wake up tomorrow and we're going to be the same company.'"
