#2867. NFL didn't know Puka Nacua had surgery when agreeing to suspension

The sudden decision of the NFL to suspend receiver Puka Nacua three games raises plenty of relevant questions. On Friday, a technically irrelevant question was answered.

After Rams coach Sean McVay disclosed to reporters that Nacua had surgery to repair a torn MCL suffered during a Week 9 win over the Commanders, Judy Battista of NFL Network reported this: "The NFL did not know Puka Nacua had had knee surgery on Tuesday when it suspended him yesterday."

Surely, that's accurate; Judy is a solid reporter. But that extra fact doesn't matter to the NFL's ultimate handling of Nacua.

The report creates the impression that, during the negotiations that resulted in the three-game suspension under the Personal Conduct Policy, Nacua and/or his representatives withheld material information. That, at some level, the league had been duped by the lack of knowledge that Nacua had undergone surgery that will sideline him for "a couple months."

First, the suspension (as the league affirmatively explained on Thursday) is separate from the injury. The NFL listed multiple examples of players who served suspensions while injured.

Second, whether Nacua (who had already missed one of the four minimum games he's required to miss while on IR), was already going to serve the entirety of the suspension while on injured reserve with or without surgery.

Third, the surgery and the extended absence actually avoids the possibility that Nacua would have returned to action exactly one game after finishing his suspension, resulting in no net games missed due to the suspension.

The mere existence of the report represents an implicit acknowledgement by the league that the negotiated suspension, aimed at allowing Nacua to double-dip when it comes to his stint on injured reserve and his suspension, underscores the broader question of whether players should be allowed to serve a suspension under the Personal Conduct Policy when otherwise unable to play. That's a separate issue for the NFL to revisit, if it chooses, through a potential revision to the terms of the policy.

As explained on Friday, the league could have refused the deal. It could have insisted on pushing the issue through the full process, with Judge Sue L. Robinson conducting a hearing and the outcome potentially being appealed (by Nacua and/or the league) to the Commissioner. But that would have entailed a non-zero-percent chance that Nacua wouldn't have been suspended at all.

For Nacua, the goal was to get the suspension served while injured. The fact that he had surgery before the agreement was reached gave him more of a buffer than the NFL knew about, expanding the window during which the suspension could be fully served before the end of the 2026 season without Nacua missing time due to the suspension. Which would still allow Nacua to have this issue behind him before the 2027 offseason, when he signs a new contract with the Rams, has the franchise tag applied, or becomes a free agent.

Of course, Nacua now needs to tread lightly. The league has made it clear that "any further violation of the Personal Conduct Policy, including failing to satisfy the conditions of his diversion program, will subject him to additional and more substantial discipline, including banishment from the League." To the extent that the league isn't thrilled by the perception/reality that it allowed Nacua to serve his suspension at a time when he otherwise could not play, the league could be ready to pounce on any opportunity to impose a longer suspension that would apply when Nacua is healthy again.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2866. Tech outages are fueling a new doomsday economy

Americans are shopping like there's no tomorrow.

A spate of high-profile technology outages that grounded planes, halted email access and slowed bank transactions have left many people worried about artificial intelligence-driven cyberattacks and the state of the U.S. energy grid.

Government officials have dismissed rumors and speculation circulating online that there could be a nefarious culprit for the series of malfunctions. Despite those assurances, consumers like Sabrina Cepeda are readying for a larger-scale technology blackout or other emergency event.

In recent days, the Houston resident has purchased propane and lighter fluid for cooking and heating equipment that can be used without electricity. The mother of three also replenished her backup stockpiles of non-perishable foods, hygiene products and allergy medicine in the event that her family is homebound for several days.

"I have a way to keep my family fed, clean, hydrated, warm and safe," said Cepeda, 39. "You want to have these things and not need them, rather than need them and not have them."

The doomsday economy is catching a bid. Wall Street and retailers are taking note.

At least half a dozen major technology outages took place in the U.S. between Nov. 12 and Nov. 17. In that time frame, spending at brands specializing in emergency products spiked about 400% compared with the same period a year prior, according to card data analyzed exclusively for CNBC by Consumer Edge.

At Ready America, which sells products like emergency survival kits, prepackaged water and high-calorie food bars, sales through e-commerce platforms have seen a roughly five-fold increase in recent days. In addition, corporate customers who keep the Vista, California-based company's supplies in their offices in case of emergency have called in to order more.

The company has rarely seen this much demand since the Covid pandemic sent Americans into lockdown more than six years ago, said Ready America President Trevyn Reese. As a result, Ready America asked its suppliers to scale up production. The family-owned business has faced a shortage of water purification tablets, and redirected shoppers to alternative offerings like prepackaged water pouches.

"With everything going on in the news right now, we've seen a large spike of people wanting to get prepared," said Reese.

While the string of outages brought power-related concerns to a boiling point, data shows that worries had been bubbling under the surface for weeks.

Shoppers began significantly ramping up buying from emergency preparedness brands late last month, according to Consumer Edge. Around that time, Fox News reported that the Pentagon was preparing U.S. military bases to operate for at least 90 days without external support. Exacerbating those fears was the ongoing war with Iran that President Donald Trump signaled could ramp back up.

Firearm trade group NSSF said that gun sales rose about 8% in October from the same month a year prior. Retailers and gun manufacturers have anecdotally reported that demand has remained elevated into November, which NSSF said can be attributed to a combination of uncertainty tied to the midterm election and the recent outages.

"If people are investing in firearms and ammunition, they're telling you very directly, with their wallet, what their priority is," said Mark Oliva, NSSF's managing director of public affairs. "You're literally investing in a product that's going to be able to protect your life and protect the lives of your family."

Now, the doomsday talk has begun making its way to Wall Street.

Mizuho told clients on Monday that consumer worries about the outages — coupled with what the bank called "apocalyptic concerns" swirling on social media — drove shoppers to stock up on emergency supplies at Costco. As a result, Mizuho said it expects a key measure of non-fuel sales growth for Costco in November to rise above 10% for the first time since 2022.

And RBC said so-called doomsday prepping has had a "real impact" on buying behavior around the globe over recent weeks. Still, the Canadian bank reported that the trend has so far boosted sales for only a small basket of items, such as shelf-stable proteins, water purification products and dry-pantry foods.

Clients of Richard McWhorter, a private wealth advisor at SRM, have been rushing to Costco to buy goods in bulk and splurging for power generators. McWhorter said he's also fielded questions about how to prepare for a situation in which the financial system goes down.

"I've never seen anything like this in my life," McWhorter said. "There's the financial side of what I do, and then there's the psychological side. And the past two weeks have been much more psychological."

Metal dealer American Hartford Gold reported an uptick in customer inquiries this week. Company president Max Baecker said he heard from some customers that their interest in products like gold — long considered a safe-haven asset during times of turmoil — was driven by newfound concern about being overly reliant on digital financial networks following a glitch at JPMorgan Chase that affected customer transactions for several hours.

Vice President JD Vance abruptly canceled his appearance at a New York Times summit on Wednesday to attend meetings about the technology outages. A day prior, Jay Clayton, the U.S. director of national intelligence, told CNBC that the recent outages were not a cyberattack or a coordinated effort by a foreign nation.

Still, Sen. Mark Warner, D-Va., told CNBC on Wednesday that people were "freaked out" by the outages. Warner, who acts as the vice chairman of the Senate Intelligence Committee, said that the U.S. was "lucky" to have not yet seen a major AI-generated cyberattack or crisis due to AI agents going rogue.

Investors may begin pushing companies to accelerate their cybersecurity spending out of an abundance of caution around rogue AI agents following the outages, according to Macro Hive CEO Bilal Hafeez. Such a situation would be reminiscent of the antivirus software push seen during the dot-com boom, he said.

The Global X Cybersecurity ETF (BUG) has climbed about 2.5% over the past two weeks, bucking the downtrend seen among AI stocks and the broader technology trade. The Nasdaq Composite on Friday fell into negative territory for 2026, putting the tech-heavy index on track for its first losing year since 2022.

To be sure, not everyone on social media has bought into the idea that they should be prepping for a doomsday scenario. In satirical videos posted on TikTok, users joked that they were gearing up by placing orders in the Starbucks drive-thru or purchasing significant quantities of alcohol.

Still, the topic appears to be on the minds of a growing number of Americans. U.S. Google searches for the term "prepping" have more than doubled when comparing the past month with the same period a year prior. On YouTube, queries for videos about "survivalism," a term describing the practice of preparing for emergencies, have soared by more than 5,000% over the same time frame.

Cepeda, the Houston mom, sees emergency prep efforts as a way to be safe rather than sorry. She's previously tapped into backup food stashes during extreme weather events like Hurricane Harvey in 2017 and Winter Storm Uri in 2021.

But this year is the first time that she's felt she needed to be prepared for a potential emergency caused by technology outages, she said. Cepeda and her husband have ensured they have enough cash on hand and fuel in their cars if there's a broader hit to the U.S. power grid, she said.

"We rely so much on technology," Cepeda said. "You need to be able to do these things the more traditional, old-fashioned way."

#2865. Gritty road trip was enough to give Knicks their mojo back

The Knicks left for their four-game road trip a bit unsettled, with questions circling them due to a rocky start to the season. After going 3-1 on the trip and beating potential playoff teams in the Raptors (twice) and Timberwolves, they return as a side rounding back into form and a better team than when they left.

"I feel like we're getting into a good rhythm offensively, defensively," Josh Hart said after the team's 102-99 win over the Timberwolves on Thursday night in Minneapolis. "I think you'll see some of the defensive rotations were on point. Obviously, we can be better. But we're continuing to learn, gain experience. Every year is different, every team is different. So I think we're doing that, and I think we're getting better.

"Whenever you win in the league, you continue to build confidence."

It is notable that they won in different ways: through free-flowing offensive showings; through grittier defensive efforts; and on Thursday, a combination of both. None of the three wins were blowouts — each game was on the line in crunch time.

New York Knicks center Karl-Anthony Towns looks on after an NBA basketball game against the Minnesota Timberwolves in Minneapolis, Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

"It shows that we're able to win in different ways," Hart said. "And that's what you have to do in the league. Some days, you're gonna shoot the cover off the ball and you can win by 10, 15, 20. Other games, it might be a possession game and offensively it's not really clicking, not knocking down shots, but you're getting stops defensively and you're able to pull out stops like that. I think it shows that this team can win games in different fashions. And that's the most important thing in the league."

A road trip like this can be beneficial, particularly early in the year as a new group gels. Despite all the continuity the Knicks have, they have been adamant that chemistry has to be reestablished.

They begin a five-game homestand Saturday, in which they'll host the surprisingly strong Trail Blazers, get a rematch with the Pacers (who could have back injured Tyrese Haliburton), play the Cavaliers for the third time already this season, see Giannis Antetokounmpo and the Heat for the first time and square off with the Hawks, who have started this season well after the Knicks eliminated them from the first round of the playoffs.

"For us to have a great road trip like this and win in so many different ways, it [says] a lot about our team," Karl-Anthony Towns said. "It's great when you can find a way to win. At the end of the day, it's hard to win in the NBA. I've learned that. It's hard to win. And you just have to find a way to win every single night. For us on this road trip to continue to find ways to win, it speaks to this team, and it's the most important thing when you play."

The Knicks' injury-free run came to an end.

New York Knicks forward OG Anunoby dunks during the first half of an NBA basketball game against the Minnesota Timberwolves, Thursday, Nov. 19, 2026, in Minneapolis. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

Jordan Clarkson (right plantar fasciitis) is out for Saturday's game against the Trail Blazers at Madison Square Garden. OG Anunoby (left calf tightness) is listed as questionable.

They might also get a break as Blazers star Deni Avdija is listed as questionable with a left foot contusion. He has missed the Blazers' last two games. Ja Morant remains out with a hamstring injury.

Some top moments since the venue opened in 1972:

The most unforgettable game in Arrowhead history came on Jan. 23, 2022, when Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes hit Travis Kelce for an 8-yard overtime touchdown to beat the Buffalo Bills 42-36 in the AFC Divisional Round — a finish that later won an ESPY as ESPN's "Game of the Year."

The venerable stadium just east of downtown also set a Guinness World Record in 2014, when crowd noise hit 142.2 decibels during the Chiefs' "Monday Night Football" rout of the New England Patriots.

How he got there: Signed a one-year, $1.2 million contract (up to $5 million in incentives)2026 stat to know: 128.3% coverage DVOA (not enough snaps to qualify but higher is worse)Comparable 2025 stat: 10.8% coverage DVOA

#2863. S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off

Esteemed Wharton professor Jeremy Siegel was more animated than usual on Friday afternoon... with good reason.

The normally staid author of "Stocks for the Long Run" spoke to CNBC on Friday as the market careened toward some ugly milestones. The S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average closed in correction territory, off more than 10% from their records. The Nasdaq Composite lost its gain for the year and the more concentrated Nasdaq-100 entered bear market territory on an intraday basis.

"These things are like straws that break the camel's back. You know what I mean? Everyone [is] all, 'I don't care, I don't care cause everything else is good' and when those straws keep on going... Bang," said Siegel.

Just a month-and-a-half ago, the S&P 500 and the Nasdaq Composite were at record highs. But the straws were already adding up by then and a market in denial is just now recognizing them.

Here are the reasons behind the sell-off:

The breaking point came this week in the form of the initial public offering of artificial intelligence leader Anthropic and a pick-up in the selling intensity for one-time AI chip leader Nvidia.

Anthropic upped its offering amount a day before going public despite the already dimming climate around AI-related stocks. The move ensured it would be the biggest IPO ever, but played into the fears that this debut — following SpaceX's monster offering over the summer — was a collective cash-out moment for the AI complex that will leave the investing masses holding the bag.

In other words, it could be the ultimate "sell the news" moment for the AI trade.

On Thursday, Anthropic traded from its $83 IPO price to a high above $115 before instantly rolling over and taking the AI trade with it. Anthropic's sell-off intensified Friday, and when the dust settled Anthropic closed the week at $88.91.

The Nasdaq posted its worst week since the Covid plunge of March 2020, down 10%.

Nvidia fell 14% on Friday with the selling intensifying after a Reuters headline hit the tape that the chipmaker's accounting is the focus of a Securities and Exchange Commission probe.

Nvidia is now down nearly 40% in November alone, a month that saw the AI bull market's leading salesman, Jensen Huang, resign as its CEO because of an affair with an alleged Chinese spy that raised speculation about whether the company's chips were ending up in Beijing's hands despite government restrictions.

To start the week, the Commerce Department tightened restrictions on U.S.-made chips to stop China from accessing them remotely from other countries.

A solid earnings report on Tuesday fell flat as Nvidia's board definitively ruled out any chance Huang would return, disappointing some of the stock's fanatics still holding out hope for his far-fetched return. Nvidia ended the week with a 23% loss.

The S&P 500 traded below its 200-day moving average — what's considered a measure of a security's long-term trend — on Tuesday and made a convincing break of it on Thursday. This turned many on Wall Street— like commodity trading advisors — into automatic sellers on the market. So-called CTAs are individuals or organizations that advise others on trading futures and swaps across a range of assets.

"We know there's a lot of trend riders on these markets," said Siegel. "They have their trend lines. They've ridden it up and as soon as it goes down, they're selling and they're selling and they're selling and they just perpetuate" the decline, he said.

The Iran conflict has caused the price oil to surge and while it's off its highs it still remains above $83 a barrel, while the fees for transiting the Strait of Hormuz and the rest of the region keep persistent pressure on global prices.

A series of numbers earlier in the month showed inflation is still far from the Federal Reserve's 2% target, sparking a run of central bank officials to warn they may have to get more aggressive with interest rates.

After increasing rates in September by a quarter point, the Fed skipped a hike in late October and sparked concern among bond investors it may be falling behind the inflation fight. The 10-year Treasury yield subsequently jumped above 5% and stayed there until a flight of safe haven fixed income buying brought it back below that level on Friday.

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said this week that the Fed may need to do a half percentage point hike next month to catch up.

"I'm very, very concerned about inflation. It's kind of headed in the wrong direction, and it's spreading out," Waller told CNBC.

"Don't fight the Fed" has long been a maxim on Wall Street and right now expectations are that the central bank will need to raise rates in December and again in January.

And late-breaking on Friday was a report from the Financial Times that the Trump Administration is considering restarting strikes on Iran.

Before the grim market milestones were met on Friday, it had long lost its mojo on fears that the pace of growth driven by AI companies was slowing.

As Anthropic prepared to go public, its third-quarter figures released earlier this month showed revenue growth last quarter was 47%, down from 144% in the second quarter.

AI momentum trades, like the VanEck Semiconductor ETF and the iShares MSCI USA Momentum Factor ETF, all topped out during the summer.

The fears of slowing growth picked up this month after the Democrats won control of Congress and key governorships, fueling speculation that states — or even the whole country — could implement stricter rules on data center construction.

CoreWeave, which builds data centers using high amounts of debt, is down 71% from its high. Caterpillar, which has reinvented itself as an AI play because of data center construction and generators, is down more than 50% from its high.

The view of Siegel and many market observers is that the economy remains in good enough shape that this will just be a run-of-the-mill correction that resets a bull market that had raised people's expectations too high because of its early ferocity.

The S&P 500 returned more than 20% in 2023 and 2024 and then gained 16% last year.

"The average over long periods for the stock market is 8%, 9% a year up," said Siegel. "People began to think that 15 to 20 [percent] was going to be the norm with AI and we have to bring that down to reality."

The only problem with that theory is that the stock market and its wealth effect was one of the major factors underpinning the economy. Stocks are a greater source of household wealth — especially for baby boomers — than they ever have been before.

With the S&P 500 clinging to a 1% gain for the year, this stock market rout could end up tipping off a virtuous cycle that slows the economy, and in turn, leads to bigger equity losses.

#2862. The Day After the Day After: Houston Texans stomp the Indianapolis Colts on Thursday Night

The Textbook Texans' Sequence: If the Texans could script their ideal game sequence, look no further than what happened between the 11:55 and 4:19 marks of the 3rd Quarter. It started with Kai Kroeger, whose punting has been sporadic at best, uncorking a 56-yard punt that landed out of bounds at the IND 5. This put the Colts in bad field position, allowing the Texans' defense to go on the attack. Two plays yielded only 2 yards for the Colts. On 3rd and long, the Colts wisely thought to double Will Anderson Jr. However, Anderson soundly beat that double team, all but driving the tackle right into Jones, as Anderson turned that sack into a safety. On the subsequent possession, Houston used a Smith 32-yard kick return to start at their 43. From there, Houston used a 10-play (6 run/4 pass), 57-yard drive that took over 6 minutes off the clock and ended in a TD that put the Texans up 23. Strong special teams, dominant defense and a steady, scoring offense. When a team is 4-6, that usually means the ideal hasn't happened a lot.

The End of the Jones Era: The Texans' defense beating of the Colts was so severe, it reverberated beyond the gridiron. As the second half wore on, sideline cameras showed visible frustration between QB Daniel Jones and HC Shane Steichen, as the Colts proved unable to move the ball. After an 87-yard, 3-point 1st quarter, the Colts only managed 80 yards and no points over the next 3. On the Colts' final offensive possession, Jones gave way to Anthony Richardson. Might the Colts face a QB controversy for their next game?

Guess not. Not 18 hours after the final whistle in Houston, the Colts announced they cut Daniel Jones. The same QB who led them to an 8-2 start last season and whom was signed to a 2-year/$88M contract this past offseason. The same QB who the Colts did not want to ship to Minnesota or Baltimore before last week's trade deadline, who would have offered some draft compensation. Granted, the Colts' season is the road to nowhere, but this sort of move only amplifies the missteps the planning of the franchise. They do save some cap space, but this only amplifies matters.

3:54, 1nd Quarter, 2nd and 10, IND Ball @ the HOU 38; Daniel Jones pass intended for TE Tyler Warren incomplete (pass defended by LB Jake Hansen).

At this point in the game, the Colts led the Texans 3-0 and had just ended up with a rather silly but effective interception. Mainly on the strength of a 26-yard pass play from Jones to Quez Watkins, the Colts were again in scoring position in HOU territory. After a short incompletion on 1st and 10, Jones was again in a shotgun set, taking the snap. TE Warren, the Colts most dangerous receiver at this point, lined up on the offensive right side and was running an in-breaking route that would have netted at least a Colts' first down closer to the Houston red zone. However, the LB in coverage, Hansen, read the play and got his hand out to deflect the pass. This allowed Houston to force the Colts into an obvious pass play. After another incompletion, the Colts settled for a long FG, which they missed. Houston then scored on three of the next four possessions to take complete control of the game.

Had the Colts made that completion, maybe they only go up 6-0, but they would have taken greater control. Houston, already under immense pressure from the implosion in Cleveland, might have pressed too much, leading to even more mistakes. Likely this Colts' team wasn't winning on Thursday but had Jones thrown that pass to where Warren could get it for a good completion, momentum might have favored the Colts even more. For Houston, a team that seems to produce more bad luck for itself than good, they got some good fortune for once.

3: Stroud's consecutive game streak for a number of stats: CJ Stroud's done all of the following over the past three games:

Thrown for at least 3 TDs (uh, actually, really, really good)

Now, if Stroud and the Texans could spin that into at least a 3-game or greater win streak…that would be even more helpful.

4813: Days since the Houston Texans last won a game at "Reliant Stadium". This stat has the caveat that after 2013, the Texans renamed Reliant to NRG. However, this past offseason, the Texans switched it back to Reliant. The last win at Reliant when it was Reliant: Sept 15, 2013, when the Texans came back to beat the Titans 30-24. This also snapped an 11-game losing streak at "Reliant", as Houston lost the remaining 7 games in 2013 as well as the 1st 4 of the new "Reliant" era in 2026.

RB David Montgomery: Since his 60 yard/2 TD rushing performance in Week 1, he's only rushed for 78 yards and no TDs. This game was his first since Week 5. 26 carries for 107 yards (season highs for any Texans back in a game) and 3 receptions for 17 yards did quite nicely. How invaluable would this have been against the Browns in the second half? With a potentially treacherous road slate coming up, if Montgomery can run like this, Houston may yet have reason for hope.

DE Will Anderson, Jr.: 5 Tackles/2 Solo/1 sack/2 TFLs/4 QB Hits/1 safety. Even without Danielle Hunter, Anderson almost single-handedly destroyed the Colts' offense.

RG Ed Ingram/RT Braden Smith: Maybe it was only a Colts' squad playing out the string but consider: 83 of the Texans' season-high 167 yards rushing came going to the offensive right, with the average rush going for over 5 yards/play. While a couple of the plays had Blake Fisher in as the extra blocker, the right side of the line was mainly Ingram and Smith. With winter coming, keeping up that high standard of play is a must.

DE Chris Braswell: Gets elevated from the practice squad to the active roster with a chance to make something happen for a team that could use the help, and he gets injured on the opening kickoff. It won't make up for the injury, but we'll ship a game ball his way.

SHOULD BE FORCED TO LISTEN TO CARLIE IRSAY-JORDAN'S GAME-DAY AUDIO AND HANDWRITE ON FANCY STATIONARY THE FULL TRANSCRIPT AFTER OVEREATING STALE TACO CABANA NACHOS:

Houston's Kickoff Coverage: Not everything was perfect in this game for the Texans. For the Texans, they did manage to give up a lot of yards on kickoff returns. Even on the opening kickoff, which could have been downed for a touchback to the IND 35, Colts RB/KR Seth McGowan ran it back for 40 yards, giving the Colts the ball in prime field position, allowing them to get that opening FG. McGowan gashed Houston for 117 yards on 3 kickoff returns. That put a lot of pressure on a mercurial defense. However, the defense did respond. Once McGowan left due to injury, the numbers worked out a little better, but this season has not been the best on the coverage front.

Colts PK Spencer Shrader: A TDATDA Game Ball winner the last time these two met, it was the complete opposite this time. 1-of-3 on FGs, including a momentum-killing hook of a makable 56-yarder in the 1st quarter.

With this win, Houston moves to 4-6, alone in second place in the AFC South and two games (effectively three) games back of the 7-seed Browns in the AFC. Houston gets an extended break before a defacto playoff game next Sunday against the struggling Ravens at Reliant. Kickoff slated for noon CST and can be seen on CBS/Paramount+

#2861. NFL picks: Who the experts predict to win in the Broncos-Raiders game

The Denver Broncos (7-2) are set to face the Las Vegas Raiders (4-6) in the first meeting between the teams this season. These are two clubs going in opposite directions. The Broncos are riding a five-game winning streak. Meanwhile, the Raiders have lost six of seven following a surprising 3-0 start to the year.

Denver is coming off its bye week and looking forward to facing a much more favorable schedule than it saw in the first half of the season. Fernando Mendoza, the rookie quarterback for the Raiders who was the No. 1 overall pick in the draft, will be making his second career start.

So let's ask the experts what they think about this one. Does anyone see the Raiders pulling the upset?

"Fernando Mendoza will be making his first road start, which can be tough for a rookie," wrote Prisco for CBS. "But it's also against a really good defense coming off a bye in a tough place to play. That's a real challenge. Look for the Broncos to get after him in this one. The Raiders' defense isn't playing well, so maybe it's just what Bo Nix needs. Broncos big."

"Rookie Fernando Mendoza will make his first road start against the Broncos and tight end Brock Bowers (ankle) will need to be monitored throughout the week," Bender wrote for The Sporting News. "Denver has 26 sacks, and the Broncos are coming off a bye week. The Broncos are 4-0 S/U and 3-1 ATS after a bye week under coach Sean Payton."

"The Broncos and their defense will be rested for this one, coming off a bye. That's not good for Fernando Mendoza in just his second NFL start," wrote Cluff for Arizona Republic.

"Fernando Mendoza drew the short stick in having to face a pair of top-10 scoring defenses in his first two NFL starts," Cersosimo wrote for NFL.com. "However, the Broncos aren't quite Mike Macdonald's unit in Seattle, as they rank 27th in yards allowed -- and pose an opportunity for the rookie QB to build on his debut. To do so, he'll need his supporting cast to be healthy (Brock Bowers) and show up (Ashton Jeanty). Even so, not sure that'll be enough against a Denver team fresh off the bye and riding a five-game win streak. The Broncos have made their hay late in games this season with Bo Nix leading the league with five fourth-quarter comebacks and six game-winning drives (tied). Facing a rookie QB and one of the NFL's worst defenses (31st in scoring, 30th in yards) in the confines of Empower Field at Mile High, where the Broncos have lost just one regular-season game since the start of 2025, is the perfect scenario for Nix and Co. to build an early lead and keep it."

The rest of the panel over at NFL.com (Jeremy Bergman, Gennaro Filice, Bobby Kownack and Dan Parr) all picked the Broncos to win as well. It's a game the Broncos can't afford a letdown at home, within the division. Good teams put teams like this Raiders squad away easily, and the Broncos need to do just that on Sunday.

#2860. Lakers survive in ugly game to down Kings

Another sloppy game saw the Lakers struggle to the finish line before coming away victorious against the Kings on Friday, 106-104.

LA remained in a funk not dissimilar from Wednesday's loss to the Jazz, trailing by as many as 11 points. The bench helped spark a run in the third quarter to get back into the game.

A clutch three from Quentin Grimes late pushed LA ahead by three with a minute remaining in the game. Zach LaVine had a chance to win it for the Kings at the buzzer but missed.

Matisse Thybulle got off to a hot start with two triples. Everyone in the starting lineup, minus Ziarie Williams, scored a bucket. Keegan Murray and Domantas Sabonis combined for all 12 of Sacramento's first points.

LA struggled with turnovers early, committing four. Luka Dončić started scoring and was already close to double figures with nine points. Sabonis and Murray were still leading the Kings with eight points each.

At the end of the first, the game was tied. Los Angeles was shooting better than the Kings, 52% to 44%. Luka ended the quarter with 13 points.

Walker Kessler started the second period strong, scoring four points. Zach LaVine started to heat up for the Kings, scoring four points. It was Sabonis, though, who continued to keep Sacramento in the game with 15 points.

Quentin Grimes missed his first four shots in the half, but finally scored on a layup. He had six points, mostly thanks to free throws.

The game was tied again with six minutes left in the half.

Cameron Carr had an immediate impact off the bench, scoring five points. Los Angeles struggled with their offense. They were 7-20 from the field in the quarter. Sacramento went on a 9-0 scoring run.

As a result, the Kings were up by eight at halftime.

De'Andre Hunter scored first to start the third period for Sacramento. The Kings seemed to have all the momentum as Acuff Jr. also drained a triple. LA, though, had some life as they scored six in a row, forcing Sacramento to call a timeout.

As soon as it looked like the Lakers were gaining momentum, Sacramento struck back almost immediately. Thybulle drained a three to make it a one-point game before the Kings responded with a 6-0 run.

LA started gaining more defensive momentum thanks to Jarred Vanderbilt's extra effort plays. That included a sequence in which he got three rebounds in a row that resulted in Sandro Mamukelashvili converting on a three-point play.

At the end of the third, the purple and gold were only down two.

The final frame started with a 3-pointer from Kessler that received a nice crowd pop. Collin Sexton also began to take over offensively, scoring four points. Los Angeles had kicked up their defense, and in return, their offense was flowing more smoothly.

Thybulle knocked down his fourth triple of the game, giving the team a five-point lead with 7:36 left.

Williams finally got a 3-pointer to fall after several games of struggling from behind the arc. Sexton was now the only player off the bench in double figures with 10. Hunter continued his strong play for the Kings, converting on a three-point play. He had 25 points.

At the 3:30 mark, the purple and gold were up by five.

The teams swapped big shots to either tie or take the lead. Luka was poked in the eye by Murray, but remained in the game.

The Lakers went up by two after Grimes made a pair of free throws. Hunter responded with a jumper on the other end to tie it again.

With 1:12 left, Grimes knocked down a much-needed three to put the team up by three.

Sacramento missed a shot. Luka then had the ball and turned it over. The Lakers intentionally fouled up three to put LaVine on the line and he split the pair. Kessler was then fouled after rebounding the miss but missed both free throws.

LaVine had the opportunity to win with a deep three and missed.

Luka finished with 25 points, four rebounds and eight assists. Kessler ended with 15 points and three blocks. Thybulle pitched in with 14 points. Grimes logged 13 points with five rebounds and seven assists.

Carr had five points off the bench. Vanderbilt scored four points with four rebounds and two assists. Mamu finished with nine points, nine rebounds and two assists. Sexton and Jake LaRavia combined for 16 points.

The Lakers' next game will be against the Utah Jazz on Monday at 7:00 PM PT.

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.

#2856. Columbia Falls title hopes end against East Helena

COLUMBIA FALLS — The last step proved to be the toughest yet for the Wildcats.

East Helena quarterback Bearek Shuman threw three touchdown passes and Leo Longcake rushed for 194 yards and two scores Friday night, sending the Vigilantes to their first Class A football championship with a 35-9 win over Columbia Falls.

The Wildcats finished 9-2, one win short of its first title since 2017. Coach Kelly Houle wanted to talk about the seniors who got them this far.

"They bring joy to my heart every day," Houle said. "I'm just really proud of them."

Columbia Falls erased a 16-point halftime deficit to defeat East Helena 38-35 in September. This time, the Vigilantes took a 21-3 lead into halftime and gave the Wildcats few chances to get back in it.

Longcake carried the ball 36 times, while Shuman completed 12 of 18 passes for 189 yards and added 83 on the ground himself. East Helena outgained Columbia falls 458-292 and intercepted three passes.

"I think we won the line of scrimmage," said East Helena coach J.D. Ferris, who credited his linemen for the big rushing numbers.

"They carried us all year," he said. "We leaned on them all year."

Trip Ross led the Wildcats with 70 yards on 13 carries. Banyan Johnston ran for 63 yards and completed 8 of 26 passes for 155 yards. Jory Hill caught three passes for 51 yards, and Hayden Strandberg hauled in two receptions for 33.

The Wildcats forced a punt on East Helena's opening possession, then moved out from their own 3-yard line before turning the ball over on downs at midfield. Longcake punched in a 1-yard touchdown on the ensuing possession, giving the Vigilantes a 7-0 lead with 5:53 left in the first quarter.

A long Johnston run helped Columbia Falls reach the red zone early in the second quarter, but the Wildcats settled for Ross' 20-yard field goal to trail 7-3 with 8:27 left in the half.

That was as close as the Wildcats would get.

Shuman found Parker Taylor for a 25-yard touchdown with 3:42 remaining. Conor Carpenter intercepted Johnston on the Wildcats' next offensive play, and the Vigilantes overcame holding penalties to score again.

Aiden Dalton caught a high lofted pass for a 30-yard touchdown with just 11 seconds before the break, making it 21-3.

This was familiar for both teams, but Shuman remembered the first meeting and how it slipped away.

"We shot ourselves in the foot and lost that game," he said. "Most importantly, you have to be able to play all four quarters in a football game. You have to play the whole game."

To start the second half Columbia Falls picked up a fourth-and-short on a Ross run up the middle, but the drive ended with a punt after back-to-back incompletions.

Longcake and Shuman kept the Vigilantes moving. After a high snap backed up East Helena, a 22-yard catch by Carpenter helped them recover and the clock started to tick from there. Shuman capped the drive off with a 15-yard touchdown pass to Avery Hager with 11:21 remaining in the game, stretching the lead 28-3.

"We just couldn't get out stuff going tonight." Houle said. "That happens."

Johnston scored the Wildcats' lone touchdown on a 2-yard run in the fourth quarter. The extra point was blocked and Longcake finished the scoring with a 22-yard dagger with just over three minutes left.

East Helena, which opened its doors in 2019, finished the season 10-1. Ferris noted the Vigilantes' 23 seniors had been freshmen on a team that won three games. He claimed the group had been talking about winning a championship together since arriving.

"I can't even put it into words," Shuman said. "It doesn't feel real."

Houle congratulated Ferris and the Vigilantes, then reflected on what his own seniors would leave behind. He credited them with building a player-led program and setting an example for the younger Wildcats to step up next.

"I'm just going to remember the brotherhood that they brought every day," Houle said. "It was a heck of a season."

#2855. Harden’s 51 points leads Cavs over the Pacers

James Harden put on a vintage performance, dropping an electric 51 points, Donovan Mitchell added 20 of his own, and Mario Hezonja put up a double-double off the bench to lead the Cleveland Cavaliers to a 128-119 victory over the visiting Indiana Pacers in an NBA Cup matchup. Cleveland sits just behind the Miami Heat in NBA Cup Group Stage play, while the Pacers fall to 1-2.

Harden finished 15-26 from the floor, including 7 for 11 from beyond the arc, to go with six assists and six rebounds. Oh, and in classic fashion, he was also 14-14 from the free throw line.

Cleveland now sits just over .500 at 9-8, beating an Indiana team that was still without star guard Tyrese Haliburton. The Cavs did not have forward Evan Mobley, who is undergoing treatment for a great toe sprain. Peyton Watson returned from a one-game absence due to a back injury, and provided 15 points, four rebounds, and three assists.

The two teams were neck and neck in the first half, with the Cavs holding a slight one-point advantage at the break. But it was the 37-year-old Harden who took over in the third quarter, helping Cleveland outscore Indiana 43-23 in the frame. Harden's 51-point effort, the 26th of his career, puts him past Kobe Bryant for third on the NBA's all-time 50-point game list.

"My idol, the person I watched growing up," Harden said in a postgame interview when told he passed Bryant on the all-time list.

Harden has been playing much better of late following his slow start, something that the former MVP has built into his conditioning strategy of playing into game shape. Mitchell's 20 points mostly came in the second half, but he helped apply pressure in that "Cavalanche" of a third quarter that allowed Cleveland to pull away.

While most of the attention will go toward the 50-point effort, Hezonja's ten points and ten rebounds off the bench continue a stretch of solid play. When the Cavs play well, and with energy, chances are that Hezonja is part of the reason why. He has made smart passes, rebounded the basketball well, and seems very comfortable on offense getting to his spots around the paint. It won't be the headline of this game, but Cleveland must be pleased with their signing of Hezonja this past offseason.

The Cavs also moved to 2-1 in NBA Cup play and put themselves in position to advance out of the Group Stage, just behind the Heat. Cleveland will not play this weekend, with their next game coming on Monday in a rematch against the Chicago Bulls. Tip-off is at 8:00 p.m EST from the Windy City.

#2853. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2849. Quentin Grimes' late three-pointer lifts Lakers to narrow win over Kings

The Lakers just finished a stretch during which they played four games in six days, a stretch that included back-to-back games this week, a stretch in which their mental and physical fortitude was tested.

They were not able to hold up during Wednesday night's defeat to the Utah Jazz, but the Lakers were able to bounce back with a 106-104 win over the hapless Sacramento Kings on Friday night at Crypto.com Arena behind another winning shot from Quentin Grimes.

Even though the Lakers were facing one-win Sacramento, they had to fight to the final few precious seconds to improve to 14-3 and 2-1 in NBA Cup play. They only secured the win after guard Zach LaVine missed a three-pointer just before the buzzer.

Read more: How the Lakers' Quentin Grimes wants to maintain his hot shooting, even off the bench

Lakers coach JJ Redick said he had some choice words for his players at halftime.

"I feel I did a decent job of regulating at halftime. They may have a differing opinion of that," Redick said. "Can't repeat every word I used. The main message was, 'We've got to be tougher. We've got to bring a little bit of energy. Certain nights, whether it's the team, how we're playing, the atmosphere in the arena, you can just be a little flat.' And I thought we just had to be tougher and pick up our energy. They accepted the challenge."

The score was tied 103-103 when the Lakers forced Kings rookie guard Darius Acuff Jr. into a turnover.

Grimes then hit a step-back three with 56.3 seconds left to give the Lakers a 106-103 lead.

It marked the second time this week Grimes made a winning three, the first coming off a banked-in trey Sunday at Phoenix with 32.7 seconds left — the day he said, "I called game."

Lakers center Walker Kessler scores off an offensive rebound against the Sacramento Kings on Friday. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

"I think so," Grimes said, smiling. "Luckily, didn't have to bank it in, it just kind of went in pretty good. But yeah, it feels good to get another one of those games in, for sure."

Grimes didn't have the best shooting night. He was two for seven from the field, one for five from three-point range. Grimes missed his first three-pointer in the fourth quarter.

But he was unfazed with the game on the line, knocking down a 26-foot jumper.

"Yeah, it don't matter. [You] shoot the next one. I mean, [it's a] make-or-miss league," said Grimes, who scored a career-high 47 points against Charlotte on Tuesday. "You gonna make it, you gonna miss it. Don't matter if you go 0 for 10 or you can go 10 for 10. Next one, get it up, and then luckily that was the biggest shot in the game and the shot of the night."

After Acuff missed a three-pointer, Luka Doncic turned the ball over, giving the basketball back to the Kings (1-14) with 23 seconds left.

Lakers star Luka Doncic drives to the basket against Sacramento Kings guard Zach LaVine (8) and forward Keegan Murray in the first half Friday. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

LaVine made one of two free throws to pull the Kings to within 106-104.

Then Walker Kessler, who had 15 points and seven rebounds, missed two free throws with 5.3 seconds left, leaving the door open for the Kings.

But LaVine missed his three-pointer as time expired, allowing the Lakers to escape on a night when Doncic had 25 points and Matisse Thybulle had 14.

So, it appeared that whatever Redick spewed at his team at halftime resonated.

"Yeah, I mean he yells," Doncic said. "But I'm used to it. So, got us all fired up. So, sometimes you get to yell."

Austin Reaves missed his fourth consecutive game because of a left heel bruise, but Redick said his star guard is making progress. The Lakers next play Monday night at home against the Utah Jazz and then again Friday at San Antonio. The hope, Redick said, is to get Reaves back for one of those games. Redick said Reaves had a "great day" on the court Friday and "made a lot of progress."

"We're trying to manage his pain," Redick said. "So, hopefully he'll be back in one of the next two games."

#2847. Why this year is different for 49ers superstar Christian McCaffrey

Christian McCaffrey has made it clear: If it were up to him, he'd take all the handoffs.

At 30 years old, in his 10th season, he has also been around the block enough to know that his personal desires aren't necessarily what's best for the team's ultimate goal.

Christian McCaffrey of the San Francisco 49ers leaves the field after a game against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. Getty Images

And that is something that all 53 players in the 49ers' locker room agree on:

What happens in Weeks 1 through 18 isn't how they'll judge this season. What's going to make or break a successful season is what happens from then on.

It's the same goal that this veteran-laden team has had basically going back to Kyle Shanahan's hiring 10 seasons ago.

And, like every other year, the 49ers will lean heavily on McCaffrey to get there.

What's different this time? McCaffrey may just be hitting his stride at just the right time.

The 49ers earned a lot of attention for their total offensive output in Sunday's shootout win over the Cowboys — 584 yards — as well as the portion for which McCaffrey was responsible.

The game was the Niners' most productive under Shanahan, arguably the NFL's best offensive coach, and their do-it-all running back accounted for almost 40% of it.

San Francisco 49ers running back Christian McCaffrey (23) runs for a touchdown against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

McCaffrey went off for 223 yards — 62 on the ground, 161 through the air — an impressive showing if there ever was one. It was McCaffrey's third-highest total of his career and the most yards from scrimmage a 49ers player has accumulated since Frank Gore's 244-yard game in 2009. Nobody had racked up 200-plus yards and three touchdowns besides Jerry Rice.

But here's the number that the 49ers Faithful should really care about:

That's the number of touches McCaffrey required to eclipse 200 yards. Catch-and-runs of 74 and 66 yards for scores, as well as another 16-yard touchdown scamper, will help with that.

That's the percentage of the 49ers' offensive snaps this season that McCaffrey has been on the field. In other words, almost one-third of the plays Shanahan has drawn up haven't involved McCaffrey at all. That's 31% of plays that McCaffrey isn't taking bruising hits, exerting himself or putting further strain on the ligaments and muscles that will carry the 49ers' load come January.

San Francisco 49ers running back Christian McCaffrey answers a question after an NFL football game against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Julio Cortez

That's a far cry from McCaffrey's NFL-high 413 touches last season. He was on the field for 83% of the 49ers' snaps last year, or 932 total plays.

This season, he's on pace to log more than 200 fewer plays by the end of the regular season.

The responsibility to carry the ball no longer exclusively falls on his shoulders.

"As talents and as people," Shanahan said, "I think they pair together very well."

That's not something that would have come out of the coach's mouth last season, when McCaffrey was their only trusted ball carrier (and still struggled, averaging fewer than 4 yards per carry for his first time with the 49ers). This year, he's averaging 4.5 yards per carry, his most efficient running since he was Offensive Player of the Year in 2023.

As soon as McCaffrey met the running back they drafted 90th overall out of Indiana, he knew this season had a chance to be different.

McCaffrey saw a kindred spirit in Kaelon Black. He's lived up to the billing.

San Francisco 49ers running back Kaelon Black (26) rushes for a touchdown against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

"I think Christian really respects Kaelon," Shanahan said. "I remember Christian telling me right when he got here how much of a pro that guy seemed like and seemed like he was made of the right stuff. It's because K.B., similar to Christian, he works at everything. He's very hard on himself. He's very tough and approaches each day to get better."

Shanahan called McCaffrey the "perfect mentor." In Black, he found a subject worthy of his wisdom. At 25 years old, he has a maturity not typically found in first-year players.

And as a bruising back who does his best work outside the tackles, he's the perfect backfield partner for McCaffrey. He's racked up 186 yards on 34 rushing attempts the past three weeks.

San Francisco 49ers' Kaelon Black (26) avoids a tackle by Dallas Cowboys' Caleb Downs (13) during a game in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

McCaffrey, meanwhile, has taken 38 handoffs for 169 yards over that same span. Shanahan's trust in Black has freed up McCaffrey to contribute more in the passing game, hauling in 12 passes for 241 yards to help offset Mike Evans' absence.

McCaffrey is still the 49ers' most important weapon in an offense full of them.

It's in everyone's best interests that his fuel tank is as full as possible come the playoffs.

Then again, the way Black is running, the 49ers seem dangerous no matter who's taking the handoffs.

"We love our room," McCaffrey said last week. "I think with KB, when he comes in it's such a spark. When I can come in and come out and get my wind, he goes in, balls out, comes out, gets his wind. If we can just continue to do that, man, I think we can be really special."

→ Baca artikel asli

#2846. Wisconsin football vs Purdue score, live updates, highlights, how to watch

WEST LAFAYETTE, Ind. – Wisconsin football is looking to hold onto some longstanding bragging rights in Week 12.

The Badgers have won their last 18 games against the Boilermakers, marking the longest active winning streak in a Big Ten series. They will be seeking their 19th consecutive win against Purdue – and a bounce-back win after a disastrous loss for Luke Fickell's Badgers against Maryland – on Nov. 21 at Ross-Ade Stadium.

Kickoff is set for 11 a.m. CT, and NBC will broadcast the game.

The Journal Sentinel will have live coverage from of the Wisconsin vs. Purdue game from Ross-Ade Stadium. Follow below for all the updates before, during and after the game:

Wisconsin vs. Notre DameDate: Sunday, Sept. 6 Time: 6:30 p.m.Location: Lambeau Field; Green Bay, WisconsinThe Badgers open the season with a marquee matchup in a historic NFL venue against a team with unfinished business. Marcus Freeman’s Notre Dame Fighting Irish finished 10-2 in 2025 and ended the year on a 10-game winning streak but withdrew from bowl consideration after landing on the outside of the College Football Playoff bracket. This game is part between the Badgers and Fighting Irish is part of the Shamrock Series that was originally supposed to be played in 2020 at Lambeau Field. But that game was canceled due to the COVID-19 pandemic. The two teams met a year later at Soldier Field. This is the first college game at Lambeau Field since Wisconsin played LSU in 2016.

Wisconsin vs. Western Illiniois Date: Saturday, Sept. 12Time: 6:15 p.m.Location: Camp Randall Stadium; Madison, WisconsinWestern Illinois is part of the Football Championship Subdivision. The Leathernecks have finished 4-8 in each of the last two years. Before that, they were 0-11 in consecutive seasons in 2022 and 2023. The team's last winning season came in 2017. Western Illinois, coached by Joe Davis, will play the Big Ten for a second straight season. The Leathernecks lost to Illinois, 52-3, and Northwestern, 42-7, to open the 2025 season.

Wisconsin vs. Eastern MichiganDate: Saturday, Sept. 19Time: 11:30 a.m.Location: Camp Randall Stadium; Madison, WisconsinWisconsin wraps up its non conference schedule against Eastern Michigan, which finished 4-8 overall and 1-5 away from home in 2025. The Eagles are part of the Mid-American Conference and coached by Chris Creighton, who is in his 13th year leading the program.

Wisconsin vs. Penn StateDate: Saturday, Sept. 26Time: TBDLocation: Beaver Stadium; University Park, PennsylvaniaWisconsin opens Big Ten play against the new-look Penn State program now led by Matt Campbell. He’s replacing James Franklin, who was fired during a disappointing 2025 that saw the Nittany Lions finish 7-6 overall and 3-6 in conference play. Campbell, 46, comes to Penn State after 10 years at Iowa State. He won Big 12 Coach of the Year three times. The Nittany Lions’ starting quarterback, Rocco Bech, transferred from Iowa State as well.

Wisconsin vs. Michigan StateDate: Saturday, Oct. 3Time: TBDLocation: Camp Randall Stadium; Madison, WisconsinPat Fitzgerald has rejoined the Big Ten to take over a struggling Michigan State program. The Spartans’ last winning season came five years ago. MSU finished 4-8 overall, 1-8 in the Big Ten and winless on the road in 2025. Fitzgerald spent 17 years at Northwestern from 2006-22, has the 14th most wins all time for a Big Ten coach and earned Coach of the Year in 2018.

Wisconsin vs. UCLADate: Saturday, Oct. 17Time: TBDLocation: Rose Bowl; Los Angeles, CaliforniaAfter a bye, the Badgers play a third straight opponent that has a head coach in 2026. Bob Chesney comes to UCLA after two years at James Madison. Last season, Chesney was the Sun Belt Coach of the Year and led JMU to the College Football Playoff. UCLA has finished 3-6 in conference play in each of its first two years in the Big Ten.

Wisconsin vs. USCDate: Saturday, Oct. 24Time: TBDLocation: Camp Randall Stadium; Madison, WisconsinLincoln Riley and the USC Trojans are now in Year 3 as members of the Big Ten and look to take the next step in competing for a championship. USC enters the season ranked 14th in the country after finishing 9-4 overall and 7-2 in the Big Ten in 2025. Wisconsin last played USC in 2024, the Trojans’ first year in the Big Ten. The Trojans scored 28 unanswered second-half points to roll to a 38-21 win over the Badgers.

Wisconsin vs. Iowa Date: Saturday, Oct. 31Time: TBDLocation: Kinnick Stadium; Iowa City, IowaWisconsin looks to snap a four-game losing streak in the series against Kirk Ferentz and Iowa in 2026.The last two matchups have not been competitive, losing 42-10 in 2024 and 37-0 last season. Ferentz, 71, is entering his 28th year as Iowa’s head coach. The Hawkeyes have been the model of consistency, posting 13 straight winning seasons.The Hawkeyes are coming off a 9-4, 6-3 season with a victory over Vanderbilt in the ReliaQuest Bowl.

Wisconsin vs. Rutgers Date: Saturday, Nov. 7Time: TBDLocation: Camp Randall Stadium; Madison, WisconsinWisconsin looks to remain perfect against Rutgers (6-0) in the all-time series with the Scarlet Knights. All of the wins have been by double figures. Like the Badgers in 2025, Greg Schiano's team finished 2-7 in Big Ten play.

Wisconsin vs. Maryland Date: Saturday, Nov. 14Time: TBDLocation: SECU Stadium; College Park, MarylandWisconsin looks to avenge last season’s 27–10 loss to Michael Locksley’s Maryland Terrapins when the two teams meet in 2026. It was Wisconsin’s first loss to Maryland in the series (4-1). The win was also Maryland’s only Big Ten victory all season. The Terrapins finished 4-8 overall and 1-8 in conference play.

Wisconsin vs. Purdue Date: Saturday, Nov. 21Time: TBDLocation: Ross–Ade Stadium; West Lafayette, Indiana Purdue has fallen on extremely hard times, going winless in the Big Ten in each of the last two years and 3-22 overall.Barry Odom is in his second season as Purdue’s head coach.

Wisconsin vs. Minnesota Date: Friday, Nov. 27Time: 6:30 p.m.Location: Camp Randall Stadium; Madison, WisconsinWisconsin ends the 2026 season on Black Friday as the Badgers renew their rivalry with Minnesota in the Battle for Paul Bunyan’s Axe.P.J. Fleck and Minnesota have gotten the better of Wisconsin of late, beating the Badgers the last two years and four of the last five seasons. Minnesota went 8-5 overall and 5-4 in Big Ten play in each of the last two seasons.

Wisconsin vs. Notre DameDate: Sunday, Sept. 6 Time: 6:30 p.m.Location: Lambeau Field; Green Bay, WisconsinThe Badgers open the season with a marquee matchup in a historic NFL venue against a team with unfinished business. But that game was canceled due to the COVID-19 pandemic. The two teams met a year later at Soldier Field. This is the first college game at Lambeau Field since Wisconsin played LSU in 2016.

#2845. Lakers squeak by lowly Kings to get back on track

The Lakers continue to look like a team in need of the benefits that are supposed to come from this stretch in Southern California.

Because after looking like one of the NBA's best teams for 3 ½ weeks regardless of the competition, they've struggled against some of the league's bottom-feeders during their four-game homestand.

Luka Dončić shoots a 3-point basket against the Sacramento Kings. NBAE via Getty Images

That was once again the case on Friday night, with the Lakers barely beating the lowly one-win Kings, 106-104, at Crypto.com Arena to get back on track after losing to the Jazz on Wednesday.

After Walker Kessler missed a pair of free throws with the Lakers up by two points with five seconds left in the fourth, Zach LaVine grabbed the defensive rebound and dribbled up the court before firing a 29-footer that hit the front rim to secure the victory for the Lakers.

Cameron Carr drives to the basket. NBAE via Getty Images

But until the final buzzer sounded, there was an unease about whether the Lakers were going to lose back-to-back games for the first time.

They trailed 62-54 at halftime, with a general malaise from Tuesday's overtime win over the Hornets and Wednesday's loss to the Jazz carrying over into Friday.

It showed defensively, with the Lakers allowing too many drives to the rim.

It showed with the seven first-quarter turnovers they committed, which directly led to 11 Kings points.

The Lakers continue to look like a team in need of the benefits that are supposed to come from this stretch in Southern California. NBAE via Getty Images

But the Lakers, led by Luka Dončić (25 points, eight assists, four rebounds and two steals), found enough of the groove they had been searching for in the second half to turn what was an 11-point deficit in the third into an eight-point lead in the fourth.

And they held on just long enough to ensure they entered the weekend with a 14-3 record before a two-day break before hosting the Jazz again on Monday.

#2844. Michael Pittman, Patrick Queen among Steelers questionable for Week 11

The Steelers have several players listed as questionable for Sunday’s game against the Eagles.

At the top of the list are receiver Michael Pittman and linebacker Patrick Queen.

Pittman has been sidelined by a foot injury since Week 3 and is returning from injured reserve. He was a full participant on Thursday and Friday, ostensibly putting him in a position to be activated and play.

Queen (hand) is also trending toward playing. He was limited on Wednesday and Thursday before being upgraded to a full participant on Friday.

Receiver Roman Wilson (hand), safety DeShon Elliott (knee), linebacker Nick Herbig (hip/ribs), and guard Gennings Dunker (knee) are also questionable.

Wilson and Dunker are returning from IR and need to be activated to the 53-man roster to play on Sunday.

Everyone else on the Steelers roster is set to play.

#2843. Bears Week 11 Flashbacks: Ditka’s Revenge, Butler’s Boot, Jets Shutout

Climb aboard the Gridiron Time Machine as we revisit three memorable Week 11 performances from Chicago Bears history!

The 1985 Chicago Bears entered Week 11 undefeated at 10-0, but their trip to Dallas carried a little extra significance for head coach Mike Ditka.

Waiting on the opposite sideline was Tom Landry, the legendary Cowboys coach who had brought Ditka onto his coaching staff after his playing career ended.

By the time the afternoon was over, Landry probably wished his former assistant had stayed home.

The Bears wasted little time making their presence felt. Richard Dent intercepted a deflected Danny White pass and returned it one yard for a touchdown to open the scoring. Mike Richardson joined the fun in the second quarter, returning another interception 36 yards for a score.

Chicago led 24-0 at halftime, and things weren't about to get any better for Dallas.

The Bears' defense intercepted four passes, recovered a fumble and sacked Cowboys quarterbacks six times. Dallas managed just 171 total yards, including a miserable 52 on the ground, while converting only three of its 13 third-down opportunities.

Walter Payton handled business on the offensive side, rushing for 132 yards on 22 carries. Chicago finished with 216 rushing yards and 378 total yards of offense, while Kevin Butler connected on all three of his field goal attempts.

The Bears scored another 20 points in the second half, including fourth-quarter touchdown runs from Calvin Thomas and Dennis Gentry, turning an already embarrassing afternoon into a historic beatdown.

Chicago walked out of Texas Stadium with a 44-0 victory, improving to 11-0 and clinching the NFC Central championship.

Four interceptions, two defensive touchdowns and a 44-point shutout against America's Team.

Not a bad way to remind your former boss how far you've come.

The Bears entered Week 11 of the 1990 season at 8-1, riding a five-game winning streak and looking like one of the best teams in the NFC.

A trip to Mile High Stadium against John Elway and the Denver Broncos would prove to be anything but easy.

Chicago's offense struggled to find the end zone for much of the afternoon, settling for a pair of Kevin Butler field goals before Brad Muster finally broke through with a 10-yard touchdown run late in the third quarter.

The score gave Chicago a 13-6 advantage heading into the final period.

The Broncos quarterback scrambled nine yards for a touchdown early in the fourth quarter, tying the game at 13 and setting up a tense finish.

Chicago had an opportunity to win in regulation, but Butler's 41-yard field goal attempt struck the left upright.

Not exactly the ending the Bears had in mind.

Fortunately, overtime gave Butler an opportunity to make up for the miss.

After the Bears got the ball back with favorable field position, Butler stepped onto the field and drilled a 44-yard field goal to secure a 16-13 victory.

While Butler supplied the game-winning points, Chicago's ground game did much of the heavy lifting. Neal Anderson rushed for 110 yards on 28 carries, helping the Bears pile up 202 rushing yards and control the football for more than 40 minutes.

The defense also made life difficult for Elway, recording six sacks and forcing two turnovers while holding Denver to just 108 rushing yards.

Chicago escaped Mile High Stadium with its sixth consecutive victory, improving to 9-1 on the season.

A missed game-winner, an overtime opportunity and a kicker who made sure he didn't miss twice.

Sometimes all you need is a second chance.

The 2006 Chicago Bears were already 8-1 when they traveled to the Meadowlands for a Week 11 matchup with the New York Jets.

They were also beginning to establish a familiar formula for winning football games.

Play outstanding defense, run the football and hope Rex Grossman doesn't make things too interesting.

Against the Jets, that formula worked just fine.

Neither offense managed to score in the first half, with Chicago struggling to move the football through the air and New York finding little success against Lovie Smith's defense.

Grossman completed just 11-of-22 passes for 119 yards on the afternoon, while the Bears converted only four of their 14 third-down opportunities.

Fortunately, Thomas Jones was there to keep the offense moving.

Jones rushed for 121 yards on 23 carries, helping Chicago accumulate 173 yards on the ground. The Bears finished with 284 total yards of offense, but points remained difficult to come by.

Robbie Gould finally broke the scoreless tie with a 20-yard field goal in the third quarter, giving Chicago a 3-0 lead.

Then, early in the fourth quarter, Grossman connected with Mark Bradley for a 57-yard touchdown that provided some much-needed breathing room.

That was more than enough for the Bears' defense.

Chicago intercepted Jets quarterback Chad Pennington twice, with Brian Urlacher and Nathan Vasher each recording a takeaway. Pennington finished with just 162 passing yards, and New York managed only 264 total yards despite running nine more offensive plays than Chicago.

The Jets also converted just six of their 17 third-down attempts as the Bears secured their second shutout of the season.

Chicago walked away with a 10-0 victory, improving to 9-1 and continuing its march toward Super Bowl XLI.

Ten points, 119 passing yards and another victory.

Who needs an explosive offense when the other team can't score?

Gary Baugher Jr. is a second year contributor to WCG, bringing football insight backed by over 16 years of experience in organized football and more than 30 years as a passionate fan of the game. You can follow him on Twitter at @iamcogs.
